## FILE 4: `/Users/naomiivie/Education for Equality/3. The Evidence/Econ Paper.md`

This is a brief outline/draft, not a full paper. Key content:

The World Bank states that Nigeria loses over 111% of its income because of its underdeveloped human capital. The HCI — composed of Education, Employment, and Health — measures human capital development. Models like Romer (1990), Solow (1956) and Mankiw et al (1992) demonstrate the importance of human development to economic growth.

Education is Nigeria's worst performing HCI indicator. Out of HCI max 324: Health 33/50, Education 64/188, Employment 34/87. In health and employment, Nigeria scores slightly below and above the mean for its lower-to-middle income class. Education is its most significant deviation from the HCI — largely due to school quality (318/624) and tertiary completion rate (11.3%/60%).

---

## FILE 5: `/Users/naomiivie/Education for Equality/3. The Evidence/ECONOMIC_COST_OF_EDUCATION_CRISIS_IN_NIGERIA.md`

**Compiled:** March 25, 2026

### Part 1: The HCI+ (World Bank 2026)

The HCI+ measures average human capital a child born today can accumulate over their working life (birth to age 65) across three pillars:
- Health (max 50): Adult survival (ages 15-60), fraction of children not stunted (ages 0-5)
- Education (max 188): Expected years of schooling (ages 5-18), harmonized learning outcomes, tertiary completion rate (ages 30-34)
- Employment (max 87): Labor force participation (ages 20-24 and 25-64), share employed, share in wage employment

HCI+ is additive: Health + Education + Employment = Total (max 325). Each point is approx 1% difference in future labor earnings.

**Nigeria's HCI+ Scores:**
| Pillar | Nigeria Score | Max | LMI Top Performer (75th %ile) | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Health | 33 | 50 | ~42 | ~9 |
| Education | 64 | 188 | 109 | 45 |
| Employment | 34 | 87 | ~49 | ~15 |
| **TOTAL** | **131** | **325** | **~200** | **~69** |

Education sub-indicators: Expected Years of Preschool 0.5/1.0, Expected Years of Schooling 8.7/12.0, School Quality (Harmonized Learning Outcomes) 318/625, Tertiary Completion Rate 11.3%/60%.

School quality at 318/625 barely above the 240 floor — years in school produce almost no learning.

**Nigeria's earnings deficit: 111%** — percentage of future earnings forgone due to gap between Nigeria's HCI+ (131) and best-in-class at similar income level (~201).

**Education's Share of the Deficit:**
Education accounts for ~65% of Nigeria's total human capital deficit (45 of 69 points) — larger than health and employment gaps combined.

**GDP Extrapolation:**
Nigeria GDP (2025, IMF): ~$285 billion. Total HCI+ gap: 70 points. Education gap: 45 points (64% of total). At ~1%/point, closing education gap = ~45% higher labor earnings. Labor income share of GDP (~45%): ~$128 billion. Education-specific earnings gain: 45% x $128B = ~$58 billion/year.

**Conservative range: $40-60 billion annually attributable to education deficits.** Lower bound ($40B): CSEA/IDRC micro estimate — wage premiums for OOSC only. Upper bound (~$58B): HCI+ derived, captures both OOSC and in-school-but-not-learning populations.

### Part 2: The $40 Billion Figure — TRUE Source

Nigeria loses $40 billion annually (~7.83% of GDP) in lost productivity from OOSC.

**TRUE source chain:** Original methodology: Milan, T. &amp; Burnett, N. (2014). "Exclusion from Education: The Economic Cost of Out of School Children in 20 Countries." Results for Development. Applied to Nigeria by CSEA (Centre for the Study of the Economies of Africa), funded by IDRC (International Development Research Centre, Canada). Reported secondarily by TheCable, attributed to "SBM Intelligence."

**Micro Approach ($40B, 7.83% of GDP):**

Step 1 — Direct GDP Loss from Foregone Primary Education:
- Total OOSC (% of primary-age): 30%
- % Unlikely to Enter: 74%
- % Non-Completing OOSC: 23.7%
- Wage Premium to Primary Education: 30.0% (Psacharopoulos &amp; Patrinos 2011)
- GDP Loss from Foregone Primary: 7.12% (23.7% x 30.0%)

Step 2 — Probability-Weighted Loss from Foregone Secondary:
- % Non-Completing OOSC: 23.7%
- Wage Premium to Secondary: 14.0%
- Rate of Continuation to Secondary: 43%
- Rate of Secondary Completion: 50% (assumed)
- Loss: 0.71% of GDP

Step 3 — Total: 7.12% + 0.71% = **7.83% of GDP**
At 2013 GDP (~$510B): 7.83% x $510B = ~$40 billion/year. Nigeria ranked 5th of 20 countries (behind Gambia 10.32%, Cote d'Ivoire 8.32%, Senegal 7.81%).

$40B is the annual GDP that will be lower when today's OOSC enter the workforce in ~10 years, compared to if they'd been educated. Recurring since Nigeria produces new OOSC cohort every year.

**Wage premiums used:** Primary: 30.0% (Psacharopoulos &amp; Patrinos 2011; Colclough et al. 2009). Secondary: 14.0% (Psacharopoulos &amp; Patrinos 2011). NOT from Aromolaran (2004), who found 2.5%/year for primary (much lower). The paper uses global/regional estimates.

**Macro Approach ($56B, 13.03% of GDP):** Uses cross-country regressions (Psacharopoulos and Patrinos 2011 + Barro and Lee 2010). Captures indirect impacts (lower crime, better public health, peer effects). 67% higher than micro estimate because it captures externalities.

**Cross-Country Comparison (Macro):**
Mali 18.16%, Burkina Faso 17.02%, Cote d'Ivoire 14.06%, Gambia 13.46%, **Nigeria 13.03%**, Senegal 10.19%, Ghana 1.92%.

Nigeria's OOSC cost in absolute terms "is greater than the GDP of Burkina Faso, Mali and Chad, put together."

**Currency &amp; Time Adjustment:** $40B calculated using 2013 GDP of $432B (Naira at ~NGN 160/$1). OOSC rose from 10.5M (2013) to 18.3M (2024) — 74% increase. The report notes estimates are likely higher now.

**What $40B DOES NOT cover:** The 23 million enrolled children who cannot read (70% learning poverty), adults already in workforce never educated, quality-of-education losses, university-level losses, health/crime/gender/civic externalities.

**Safe framing:** "recovering a share of the $40 billion" or "addressing the root cause of the $40 billion loss."

### Part 3: World Bank Direct Education Figures for Nigeria

HCI+ 2026 key stats: 111% of future earnings lost, Education 64/188, LMI top performer 109/188, effective 12 years schooling = +20% labor earnings, test scores ONE POINT BELOW 2010 (stagnant 15 years), tertiary completion 11.3% (vs 17.6% LMI average), gender gap 6 points (female 128 vs male 134), closing gender gap = +3% future earnings, HOPE-EDU $500M approved.

Original HCI: Score 0.36/1.0 (child born today will be 36% as productive as possible), ranked 150th of 157 countries, expected years of schooling 10.2, learning-adjusted 5 years, 5.2 years wasted, harmonized test score 309/625.

Learning Poverty (2022): 70% of 10-year-olds cannot read (updated 72.6% in 2024). Global cost: $21 trillion in lifetime earnings (17% of global GDP). Sub-Saharan Africa learning poverty: 87%. Global LMICs: 70%.

AI Tutoring RCT (2024 Edo State): 1.5-2 years of learning in 6 weeks, $48/student, benefit-cost ratio 161-260 (highest ever measured for any education intervention).

OOSC Report (2024): 18.5 million (15% of global total). Primary-age: ~10.2M. Lower-secondary: ~8.1M.

World Bank Financial Commitments: HOPE-EDU $500M (April 2025, 29M children), Total Nigeria human capital package $1.08B (April 2025), Adolescent girls empowerment $700M (2023), Universalizing basic education estimate $6.7B/year additional for secondary alone.

### Part 4: Returns to Education — Nigeria vs Global

| Education Level | Nigeria | Global Average |
|---|---|---|
| Primary school | 2-4%/year | 10%/year |
| Secondary school | 2-4%/year | 10%/year |
| University | 10-15%/year | 15%/year |
| Girls' education | Up to 10%/year | Highest-ROI development intervention |

Lifetime Earnings (starting from NGN 20,000/month, no schooling):
- No school: NGN 9.6 million over 40 years
- Primary done, can't read (6 yrs x 2%): NGN 10.8M
- Primary done, CAN read (6 yrs x 8%): NGN 15.2M
- Secondary done, low quality (12 yrs x 3%): NGN 13.7M
- Secondary done, good quality (12 yrs x 8%): NGN 24.2M
- University graduate: NGN 72-120M

**Critical insight:** Learning is worth 4x more than attendance. A child who can read earns NGN 4.4M more over a lifetime than one who can't — same years in school.

Cross-Country Comparison (12 years of school, in Naira):
- Nigeria (3%/yr): NGN 28,516/month
- India (8%): NGN 432,998
- Kenya (9%): NGN 329,543
- Brazil (10%): NGN 1,600,599
- US (10%): NGN 6,276,857

After 12 years of school, a Nigerian earns less than an Indian, Kenyan, or Brazilian with ZERO education.

### Part 5: The Scale of the Crisis

National: 33.3M enrolled in public primary, 18.3M OOSC (UNICEF 2024) / 18.5M (World Bank 2024), 70-72.6% 10-year-olds cannot read (~23 million), pipeline attrition 30M start primary -&gt; 6M reach senior secondary (80% loss), 5.2 learning-adjusted years wasted per child, system efficiency only 16% of planned learning happens (EKOEXCEL Year 5), teacher shortage 165,000+, 46% of teachers teach all subjects, 133 million (63%) in multidimensional poverty, WAEC/NECO pass rate 38.3% initially, revised to 63%.

Lagos: 703,756 OOSC (LASUBEB 2023-24), 343,000-375,000 public primary pupils (EKOEXCEL Year 5), 95% household phone ownership, 78% smartphone penetration, ~22% feature phone households (~710,000 HH), ~99% 2G coverage, 128 million without mobile internet (national, 54.6%), Sabi reachable children (Lagos) 484,000-684,000.

Mobile &amp; Internet: 93% national phone ownership, 45.4% internet penetration, 128M not using internet, 120M mobile internet usage gap despite coverage, ~40 percentage point usage gap, bottom-decile data affordability 1.75-2.33% of income for 1GB (exceeds ITU 2% threshold).

### Part 6: What Works — Intervention Evidence

| Intervention | Country | Result | Cost | BCR |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AI tutoring (World Bank RCT) | Nigeria (Edo) | 1.5-2 years in 6 weeks | $48/student | 161-260 |
| Rori AI (WhatsApp math tutor) | Ghana | 0.37 SD (1 extra year) | $5/student/year | — |
| ConnectEd (phone tutoring) | 6 countries | 0.33 SD | $12/student | Top percentile |
| TaRL (Teaching at Right Level) | Madagascar | 0.56 SD (~2 years gains) | ~$10/student | — |
| EKOEXCEL (structured pedagogy) | Nigeria (Lagos) | Non-readers 38%-&gt;12%, comprehension 21%-&gt;60% | $24/pupil/year | — |
| XPRIZE tablets | Tanzania | Reading 7%-&gt;45% in 15 months, no teachers | — | — |
| EIDU (digital personalized) | Kenya | 0.52 SD | — | — |
| Jolly Phonics | Nigeria | 60% increase word recognition, 72,127 pupils | — | — |
| **Sabi (projected)** | **Nigeria** | **Expected 0.25-0.35 SD** | **$0.50-5/student/year** | **—** |

Cost-Effectiveness: AI tutoring BCR 161-260 (highest ever), Technology-assisted learning ~63:1, Improving school quality 17:1, Best programs globally 3 additional learning-adjusted years per $100/child.

### Part 7: Key Figures for Grant Applications

**The Killer Stat:** "Nigeria's education deficit alone accounts for two-thirds of the country's total human capital gap — more than health and employment combined. The World Bank rates Nigeria's education system at 64 out of 188 — barely half of what countries at the same income level achieve. Closing this gap would increase labor earnings by ~45%, worth an estimated $40-60 billion annually."

Headline numbers: $40B/year (conservative, OOSC only), ~$58B/year (comprehensive, HCI+ derived), education's share ~64% of total deficit, +20% labor earnings from 12 effective years, 111% total human capital deficit, 0.36 HCI, 18.3M OOSC + 23M in school but not learning, AI tutoring achieves 1.5-2 years in 6 weeks, Sabi's cost $0.50-5/student vs $48 (World Bank) vs $24 (EKOEXCEL), World Bank investment $500M HOPE-EDU + $700M girls = $1.2B, global learning crisis cost $21 trillion.

## FILE 6: `/Users/naomiivie/Education for Equality/5. Cost &amp; Feasibility/EMBEDDED_MARKETING_RESEARCH.md`

**Compiled:** March 31, 2026

### Part 1: What Is Embedded Marketing?
Product placement and branded content integration is a $23 billion industry globally ($33 billion by 2024).

Three models: Sponsored Content ("This lesson is brought to you by MTN"), Branded Educational Content (brand woven into genuinely useful content — e.g. Safaricom M-PESA Financial Literacy Kit, Lifebuoy EUR 30M Handwashing Program across 5+ countries, Colgate Bright Smiles across 80+ countries), and Edutainment Integration (Sesame Street model — PNC Bank sponsors financial literacy segments, LEGO Foundation sponsors play-based learning, Sanlam sponsors South African version; Ubongo in Tanzania funded through donor grants, corporate sponsorships, government partnerships).

### Part 2: Telco-Sponsored Education Programs in Africa

**Viamo 3-2-1:** Free IVR in 16 countries. MNOs absorb airtime as CSR. $12 billion in cumulative free minutes. Airtel alone contributed $3.2M (25 years of free airtime). 5-8% of subscriber base uses it regularly when mature. Telcos do it for subscriber retention, regulatory goodwill, brand differentiation, and low wholesale cost.

**Airtel-UNICEF:** $57M partnership across 13 African countries — zero-rated education platforms, school connectivity, teacher training.

**Safaricom + Eneza (Shupavu291):** USSD/SMS learning, 10.2M customers, $3.5M revenue (2023). Carrier billing: students pay KSh 10/week ($0.08) from airtime. Revenue share: 75%/25% (content provider/Safaricom) at low price points, 65/35 at medium, 50/50 at high. Safaricom takes smaller cut for education because it reduces churn.

**Vodacom Siyakha (South Africa):** Zero-rated education, 7.5M users. Part of B-BBEE compliance.

**Vodacom e-School:** Zero-rated full South African curriculum. Vodacom pays everything.

**MTN Nigeria VAS Revenue:** NGN 70 billion+ in VAS revenue (2021). Standard Nigeria VAS revenue split: 60% MNO / 40% content provider (NCC-negotiated standard).

### Part 3: Zero-Rating — Three Models

**Model A: Telco Absorbs Cost as CSR** — No money changes hands. Telco volunteers. Examples: Wikipedia Zero (78 operators, 67 countries, ended 2018), Vodacom e-School, Viamo 3-2-1. **This is the model Sabi should target initially.**

**Model B: Content Provider Pays Telco ("Sponsored Data")** — Facebook Free Basics, Airtel Zero (India). **Not recommended for Sabi** (defeats nonprofit purpose).

**Model C: Advertising-Supported Zero-Rating** — Service zero-rated but users hear ads. MTN Ads delivers 100M+ impressions/month, 4.4% CTR (far above ~1% industry average). **Secondary model for Sabi** — pre-lesson sponsor tags generate revenue.

### Part 4: What Telcos Actually Care About

- **Churn Reduction:** 5% retention increase = 25-95% profit increase (Bain). Acquiring new subscriber costs 5-7x more than retaining. Average African prepaid churn: 20-70%. Viamo found 5-8% of subscribers use education regularly and churn less.
- **ARPU:** Engaged VAS users have higher ARPU. MTN Nigeria VAS drives NGN 70B+ annually.
- **Regulatory Goodwill:** NCC is pushing telcos to zero-rate education (recommendation, not yet binding). South Africa B-BBEE essentially mandates social investment.
- **Brand Perception:** "The network that teaches your children to read" is powerful differentiation.
- **Subscriber Acquisition:** "Join [Network] and your child gets free tutoring."

### Part 5: Embedded Marketing Models for Sabi

1. **Pre-Lesson Sponsor Tags (Immediate):** 5-10 second audio before lesson. At 1,000 students x 5 calls/week x 4 weeks = 20,000 impressions/month. Precedent: Rising Academies' Rising On Air, Ubongo's TV shows.

2. **Branded Math Problems (Natural Integration):** "Amina goes to the MTN shop to buy 3 airtime cards for NGN 200 each..." Brand IS the learning material. Nature 2025 study: children solve math 9x more often with market-contextualized problems. Using real brand names makes it MORE realistic.

3. **Branded Modules (Sponsorship):** "The MTN Counting Module." Large one-time or recurring fee. Precedent: Sesame Street's PNC Bank financial literacy, LEGO Foundation play-based learning.

4. **Telco Zero-Rating for CSR (Core Model):** MTN/Airtel/Glo absorbs NGN 15/min callback cost. Precedent: Viamo ($12B in free minutes), Airtel-UNICEF ($57M), Vodacom e-School, Wikipedia Zero.

5. **Carrier Billing (Revenue Share):** For premium/adult courses. Nigeria standard: 60% MNO / 40% content provider (NCC standard). At education price points, may negotiate better (Safaricom gives 75/25 at low price points).

### Part 6: Nigeria-Specific Regulatory Context
- NCC recommends (not yet mandates) telcos zero-rate education. Creates regulatory tailwind.
- VAS licensing: NGN 10,000,000 (~$6,250) or partner with existing VAS aggregator.
- Post-devaluation telco reluctance — frame Sabi as churn reduction, not just CSR cost.

### Part 7: Voice-Specific Advantages
1. Voice cheaper to zero-rate than data (minimal bandwidth vs streaming video).
2. Feature phone reach is unique — works on NGN 3,000 ($5) phone.
3. No competing with OTT (YouTube, TikTok, WhatsApp). 100% engagement.
4. Measurable per-call impact — exact data for CSR reports.

### Part 8: Recommended Revenue Stack
| Revenue Source | When | Who Pays | Expected Revenue |
|---|---|---|---|
| Donor/grant funding | Now – Year 2 | Funders | $50K-$500K grants |
| Telco CSR zero-rating | Year 1-2 | Telco absorbs | Saves $3-23/student |
| Pre-lesson sponsor tags | Year 1+ | Advertisers | Per-impression pricing |
| Branded math problems | Year 1+ | Brands | Product placement fees |
| Branded modules | Year 2+ | Large sponsors | $5K-$50K per module |
| Government contracts | Year 2-3 | LASUBEB, State/Federal MoE | $5-24/pupil |
| Adult courses (carrier billing) | Year 2+ | Users (NGN 500-1,000/course via airtime) | 40% after MNO cut |
| Premium tutoring (AP/IB/IGCSE) | Year 3+ | Parents | Higher margins |

**Key insight: The children's service is free forever. Revenue comes from sponsors, telcos, governments, and premium products — never from the families Sabi serves.**

**Global CPM benchmarks:** Programmatic audio $5-$15, Podcast pre-roll (30 sec) $15-$30, Podcast pre-roll (host-read) $18-$25, Podcast mid-roll (host-read, 60 sec) $25-$50, Post-roll $10-$20, Custom branded segment up to $150, Average across podcast verticals $25.46.

**MTN Ads:** 100M+ monthly impressions. 4.4% CTR (vs 0.3% industry average — 14.7x better). Device-agnostic. Estimated CPM: $2-$8. Clients: Unilever Nigeria, WHO, First Bank, BETKing, Old Mutual. Contact: mtnsales@alephholding.com.

**Airtel Ads:** Africa's first integrated telco DSP. 27 billion weekly impressions, 23.5M in-app daily impressions, 200M+ daily SMS notifications.

Program sponsorship: NGN 98,000-1,000,000+ ($61-$625+). Total Nigeria radio ad market: $50.1M (2025 forecast).

**Ubongo (Tanzania):** Total revenue (2018) $1.3M — 83% grants, 4.3% B2B sales, 1.8% advertising/sponsorship (~$23,400), 0.5% royalties. LEGO Foundation grant: $27.8M for Akili Family program.

### Parts 11-16: Revenue Projections &amp; Pricing Justification

**At Pilot Scale (1,000 students):** 20,000 impressions/month. At $1.50-$4.00 CPM: $30-$80/month. Negligible — value is proving the model.

**At Scale (100,000 students):** 2M impressions/month. At $1.50-$4.00: $3,000-$8,000/month. At premium $8-$15 CPM (justified by 100% listen-through, captive, no skip): $16,000-$30,000/month. Plus module sponsorships $40K-$500K one-time.

**At National Scale (1,000,000 students):** 20M impressions/month. At premium $8-$15 CPM: $160,000-$300,000/month ($1.9M-$3.6M/year). Comparable to Eneza's $3.5M annual revenue.

**The Unskippable Audience Premium:** Non-skippable formats command 2-3x higher CPMs. Captive audience CPMs: Standard digital video $5-$15 (30-40% completion, 76% skip), CTV non-skippable $20-$65 (90-97%), Cinema $20-$70 (~95%), Rewarded video $10-$50 (95%+), Podcast mid-roll baked-in $35-$55 (~100%), Pandora unskippable $8-$12 (~100%), **Sabi voice call: TBD, 100% completion, 0% skip.**

Cinema 4-week national campaign: $100K-$250K+. Gas Station TV CPM: $15-$40. Airport CPM: $46.69. Host-read ads outperform producer-read by 31% in purchase rate.

**Native Advertising Premium:** Native ads command 30-40% higher CPM than standard display. 50-60% higher engagement. Branded content produces 86% brand recall and is 22x more effective than traditional ads (Nielsen).

**Education-Specific:** Duolingo total revenue $531M (2023), advertising revenue $49.86M (2023). COPPA opt-in reduces ad revenue 40-60% for children's content. Sabi's model (contextual, not targeted) is COPPA-compliant by design.

**Recommended Sabi CPM: $8-$25** for Nigerian market. Floor $8 (Pandora-equivalent), Mid $15 (native integration + captive), Ceiling $25 (host-read baked-in podcast equivalent, adjusted for Nigerian market).

At 100,000 students x 5 calls/week x 4 weeks = 2M impressions/month:
- At $8 CPM: $16,000/month ($192K/year)
- At $15 CPM: $30,000/month ($360K/year)
- At $25 CPM: $50,000/month ($600K/year)

## FILE 7: `/Users/naomiivie/Education for Equality/SABI_PATH_TO_SCALE.md`

**Core Principle:** Focus on kids first. API business is year 2-3. Every buyer will ask "show me the data." Build the proof, then build the platform. Every platform started as a product. Twilio was an internal tool. Stripe built payments for themselves first. Sabi teaches kids first, proves it works, then extracts the pipeline into an API.

### Phase 1: Prove It Works (Now -&gt; Q4 2026)
- 10-kid think tank: April 15, 2026, Lagos, 2 weeks. Test diagnostic placement, voice clarity, market-math framing, pacing. Key metric: do kids call back the next day without being told to?
- Fix what breaks: May-June 2026
- Legal setup: June-Aug 2026. 501(c)(3) filing, Nigerian CAC registration.
- Remaining lesson scripts: June-Aug 2026. Literacy Phase 2, Numeracy Modules 5-6.
- School partnerships: June-Aug 2026. 10 schools across 5 high-burden LGAs via advisory board (Dr. Sonia/EKOEXCEL, Rhoda Odigboh, Remi Tanimola).
- 150-350 kid pilot: Sept 2026 - Feb 2027. Pre/post TaRL assessment. Target: 0.25-0.35 SD improvement. 5-7 min daily calls, alternating literacy/numeracy.
- Pilot results published: Feb-Mar 2027.
- **Cost: ~$2K-5K (funded by MIT prize + Wesleyan grant).**

### Phase 2: Get Funded with the Data (Q1-Q2 2027)
- Take pilot results to Mastercard, Gates, Ford, Elumelu ($100K-500K)
- Apply UNICEF Venture Fund ($100K equity-free)
- Pitch Lagos State LASUBEB ($500K-2M)
- Pitch World Bank HOPE-EDU ($1M+ from $500M fund)
- Negotiate zero-rating with MTN/Airtel
- Pitch bilateral donors: USAID, DFID, GIZ ($200K-5M)
- **One $100K grant funds a 5,000-kid deployment.** Zero-rating drops per-student cost from ~$23 to ~$1.

### Phase 3: The API Emerges Naturally (H2 2027)
At 5,000+ kids, pipeline proven on real African voices, real 2G networks, real noise. Other orgs ask "can we use your voice system?"
- Inbound demand from health NGOs, fintech, government digital services
- Wrap pipeline in API: [REDACTED:sensitive-label] architecture, API key management, usage metering, billing
- "Sabi for Developers" soft launch: invite-only, 3-5 early partners, per-minute pricing
- Pricing: $0.04-0.15/min (undercuts Vapi/Retell by 40-65%, only provider optimized for African markets)
- Engineering required: 3-6 months

### Phase 4: Two-Product Platform (2028)

**Sabi for Organizations (Managed Delivery):**
| Scale | Per-student price | Revenue potential |
|---|---|---|
| Pilot (100-500) | $10-15/student | $1K-7.5K |
| Mid-scale (1K-50K) | $5-8/student | $5K-400K |
| National (100K+) | $2-5/student | $200K-500K+ |
| State government (500K students) | $3-5/student | $1.5M-2.5M |

**Sabi for Developers (Voice AI API):**
| Tier | Per-min rate | Margin at $0.01 cost |
|---|---|---|
| Starter | $0.04/min | 75% |
| Pro | $0.07/min | 86% |
| Enterprise | $0.10-0.15/min | 90%+ |
| On-prem | $3,000-5,000/mo | Fixed |

Revenue at scale: 1 NGO partner (10K students) $70K-112K/mo, 5 partners $350K-560K/mo, Government contract (1 state) $3.5M-5.6M/mo.

### What NOT to Build Now
API infrastructure, developer docs/SDKs, analytics dashboards for partners, white-label features, adult literacy curriculum, AP/IB/IGCSE tutoring product. All real, all valuable. None of them teach kids to read. Build them after pilot data.

### The Moat
Not the lesson scripts. It's the pipeline: Nigerian-accent Whisper (fine-tuned on AfriSpeech-200, once OOM resolved), Chatterbox TTS with Naomi's voice clone + emotion-aware exaggeration, answer matcher for children's spoken responses, flash callback telephony (zero cost to family), persistent student memory across calls, market-math framing (9x better outcomes, Nature 2025), 240+ voice lesson scripts with embedded emotion tags.

### Right Now Priorities
1. Get the phone call working end-to-end for the April 15 think tank
2. Activate ElevenLabs coupon (deadline April 3)
3. Don't build API infrastructure — build the thing that teaches kids to read

The API is the business model that makes Sabi sustainable. But the kids are the business model that makes Sabi *fundable*. Right now we need funding, not customers. Put the API vision in the pitch deck. Build it after pilot data.</result>

**Speaker notes:**
"Beyond grants and government contracts, we have four revenue streams that make this self-sustaining.

First — adult courses. 45 million Nigerian adults are illiterate. Millions more want vocational skills. We upload job interview prep, financial literacy, English proficiency — same AI, same phone call, same infrastructure. Charge 500 to 1,000 naira a month through airtime billing. Even 1% penetration of 90 million phone-accessible adults generates $3 to $7 million a year.

Second — private schools. Lagos private schools pay human tutors 50,000 to 200,000 naira per student for exam prep. Sabi delivers AI tutoring at a fraction of that. 200 schools at $12 per student is $720,000 a year.

Third — the API. We've built the only hosted voice AI pipeline optimized for African accents, African languages, and 2G networks. Nobody else has this. Vapi charges $0.14 a minute and can't understand Nigerian English. We charge $0.05 to $0.08 and it was built for it. One NGO partner using our API for 10,000 students generates $70,000 to $112,000 a month at 75 to 80% gross margin. Health NGOs, fintech companies, government digital services — they all need voice AI in Africa.

Fourth — embedded marketing. Here's the beautiful thing. Our math lessons naturally use real market scenarios. 'Amina buys three MTN recharge cards for 200 naira each — how much did she spend?' That's not an ad. That's a math problem backed by a Nature study showing children solve it nine times more often than abstract math. But for MTN, that's 100% listen-through brand exposure on an unskippable voice call. At 100,000 students, that's $192,000 to $600,000 a year in marketing revenue.

By Year 3, total revenue across all streams: $1.4 to $1.9 million. Total costs: $132,000 to $162,000 — because our entire AI stack runs on GPU servers at $200 a month. That's 88 to 93% margin. The costs are almost entirely fixed. Every additional student costs essentially nothing."

### LEGAL_ROADMAP.md

**Entity Structure — Four Phases:**

**Phase 1 (Now):** Education for Equality Inc. — US 501(c)(3) Nonprofit. Receives grants/donations, develops curriculum/IP, tax-exempt, owns "Sabi" trademark.

**Phase 2 (Within 12 months):** EfE Nigeria — Incorporated Trustee via CAC. Implements free programs, receives grants from US entity, tax-exempt charitable entity.

**Phase 3 (Future — when monetizing):** EfE Nigeria Ltd — Independent Nigerian For-Profit. Sells tutoring to private schools, pays ~29% Nigerian tax, licenses curriculum from US nonprofit, NOT a subsidiary (no ownership link).

**Phase 4 (Future — if raising VC):** EfE Inc. — Delaware C-Corp. Raises venture capital, exists alongside the nonprofit, separate entity/mission.

**Phase 1 Steps:**
- Step 1: Get EIN — free, instant online
- Step 2: Incorporate as Nonprofit in Connecticut — $50-75, 1-2 weeks. Name: "Education for Equality Inc." Needs registered agent in CT, statement of purpose, at least 3 board members.
- Step 3: Establish Board of Directors — minimum 3: Naomi Ivie (Founder, Board Chair), Amber Bretz (Operations), 1 additional independent member. Requires bylaws, organizational meeting, recorded minutes.
- Step 4: File for 501(c)(3) — Form 1023-EZ ($275, 4-8 weeks). Unlocks: tax-deductible donations, foundation grants (Gates, Mastercard Foundation, etc.), US government grants (USAID, State Dept, USADF), competition prizes generally tax-exempt.
- Step 5: Open US Bank Account — free nonprofit checking.
- Step 6: Register "Sabi" Trademark (US) — $250-350, 8-12 months. Class 41 (Education) and/or Class 9 (Software/apps).
- Bridge Option: Fiscal sponsorship if needed before 501(c)(3) approval. Options: Wesleyan University, Social Impact Commons, New Venture Fund. Cost: 5-10% admin fee.

**Phase 2 Steps (Nigerian Registration):**
- Step 7: Register Incorporated Trustee with CAC — ~N70,500-100,000 (~$45-65), 2-4 weeks. Requires constitution/trust deed, 2+ trustees, registered office in Nigeria, newspaper publication.
- Step 8: Register with FIRS — free, 2-4 weeks.
- Step 9: Register with SCUML — free, 1-2 weeks.
- Step 10: Register "Sabi" Trademark with CAC — ~N50,000-100,000 (~$30-65), 6-12 months.

**Phase 3 (Revenue Generation):**
- Step 11: Register Nigerian For-Profit (LLC with CAC) — ~N20,000-50,000. Owned by Naomi personally (not by US 501(c)(3)). Avoids CFC complications.
- Step 12: Licensing Agreement — US nonprofit (IP owner) licenses to Nigerian for-profit. Fair market value. Nonprofit retains content control. Licensing fees fund the free-education mission.

**Tax Implications Table:**
- Grants/donations to US nonprofit: US, 0%, $0
- Tutoring revenue by Nigerian for-profit: Nigeria, ~29% (25% CIT + 4% Dev Levy), $0 US tax
- Licensing fees received by US nonprofit: US, 0% (program-related income), $0
- Nigerian WHT on licensing fees to US: Nigeria, 10%, non-recoverable cost
- Effective total tax rate: ~29% on Nigerian commercial income. No double taxation.

**Phase 4 (VC):**
- Step 13: Delaware C-Corp — $300-500 filing + $175-400/yr franchise tax + registered agent. High-Tax Exclusion means no double taxation (Nigeria's ~29% &gt; 18.9% threshold). This is the Andela/Flutterwave/Paystack model.

**IP Protection Summary Table:**
- "Sabi" name: Trademark (USPTO + CAC) — To file
- 286 lesson scripts: Copyright — automatic, Protected
- Curriculum structure: Copyright — automatic, Protected
- App code (curriculum-app): Copyright — automatic, Protected
- RAG knowledge base: Copyright + Trade Secret — Protected
- TaRL implementation: Trade Secret — Protected
- Persistent memory system: Trade Secret — Protected
- "Voice AI for education" concept: NOT protectable — prior art from Viamo (2012), Eneza (2011), M-Shule (2016)

**Cannot be protected:** General concept of AI tutoring over phone calls, USSD/SMS/IVR for education, Cambridge curriculum learning objectives (owned by Cambridge), Open Educational Resources used in lessons (CC-BY licensed).

**Compliance Calendar:**
- US: Form 990-N by May 15 if revenue &lt;$50K (free), Form 990-EZ if $50K-$200K. CT annual report $25-75. Board meeting minimum 1/year. Renew registered agent $50-300/yr.
- Nigeria: Annual returns with CAC within 42 days of AGM (N5,000). Annual tax return with FIRS (free). SCUML compliance (ongoing, free).

**Estimated Total Costs:**
- Phase 1 (US): $575-700
- Phase 2 (Nigeria): ~$75-130
- Grand Total: ~$650-830

**Key Legal Principles:** Expenditure responsibility, no private inurement, arm's-length transactions, independent governance.

**Address Update Checklist (when leaving Wesleyan):** File Form 8822-B, update CT Registered Agent, update CT Secretary of State, update bank. If leaving CT: hire CT registered agent service (~$50-100/yr) or re-domesticate.

**Priority Actions:** Week 1: EIN + identify 3rd board member. Week 2: CT nonprofit incorporation ($50-75). Week 3: Draft bylaws, board meeting, minutes. Week 4: File 1023-EZ ($275) + open bank account. Month 2: USPTO trademark ($250-350). Month 3-6: Begin Nigerian registration.

### SABI_COPYRIGHT_BRIEF.md

**About Education for Equality:** Edtech platform connecting underserved school-aged children in Lagos, Nigeria with education. Uses AI voice-enabled lessons for culturally relevant, free foundational literacy and numeracy instruction on phones families already own — no internet required. Website: eduforequality.org. Demo: sabi.eduforequality.org.

**What Sabi Is:** AI-powered voice tutoring system for children aged 8-14 in Nigeria through ordinary phone calls. "Sabi" means "to know" in Nigerian Pidgin English. 5-7 minute lessons, 3-5 times per week. Uses proprietary real-time AI pipeline. Returning children greeted by name and assessed. Multiple children sharing one phone each get their own learning profile. Long-term vision: deliver full curricula entirely through voice.

**Curriculum &amp; Modules:** ~240 original voice lesson calls across two courses — Foundational Literacy (5 modules, ~140 calls) and Foundational Numeracy (6 modules, ~100 calls), each preceded by diagnostic placement assessment. Designed specifically for Nigerian children aged 8-14. Includes original adaptive learning logic, diagnostic assessments, scaffolding protocols.

**Statement of Originality — Four elements:**
1. Original curriculum content: 240+ voice lesson calls across literacy and numeracy, authored entirely by E4E.
2. Original pedagogical design: Teaching methodology, adaptive logic, diagnostic assessments, scaffolding protocols.
3. Original system prompts and AI orchestration: Tutor personality, voice interaction rules, lesson flow structure, multi-model orchestration pipeline.
4. Original software: Server code, API routes, student memory system, voice processing pipeline, curriculum retrieval system.

Sabi is the product of extensive research, original curriculum development, and custom software engineering for 20 million out-of-school children in Nigeria.

### BAKAME_AI_DUE_DILIGENCE_MEMO.md

**Date:** March 9, 2026. **Classification:** Internal — Confidential.

**Entity Overview:**
- Venture Name: Bakame AI. Website: bakame.org.
- Founded: ~2024-2025 as student project at TCU.
- Co-founders: Happy Herman Niyorurema (Rwanda), Mame Diarra Niang (Senegal).

- Total Known Funding: $58,000 (prizes and grants — no VC).
- Incorporation Status: Not confirmed; no corporate filings identified.
- Country of Operation: Rwanda (planned: Senegal, Zambia).

**Funding:**
- TCU CREATE Accelerator (Shaddock Seed Fund): $8,000 (May 2025)
- Global Best M-Gov Award (Gold) — World Government Summit, Dubai: $50,000 (February 2026)

**Public Technology Claims:**
- Africanews (Feb 5, 2026): "They built their own AI (large language model), but instead of putting it on the web, we deployed it on telephony."
- TCU News (2026): "AI-powered learning assistant capable of delivering lessons, teaching concepts and tracking progress."
- TCU 360 (Oct 2025): English language lessons via phone call, individual progress tracking and spaced repetition.
- Website claims three pillars: "Learn AI" (personalized conversations, &lt;4s response, localizable, any feature phone), "Operate AI" (dashboards, metrics, compliance data, automated reporting), "Scale AI" (offline-first, multi-school/district, government/NGO integrations, high-volume telephony).

**MBAZA-NLP Connection:**
Open-source repos under GitHub org MBAZA-NLP share name "Bakame" and target Rwanda with same use case. Originally developed by GIZ, Digital Umuganda, Mozilla, RISA (Rwanda Information Society Authority), and Arxia. Original project was Mbaza COVID-19 Chatbot for public health info in Kinyarwanda via voice calls.

**Repositories:**
- `MBAZA-NLP/bakame-chatbot` — Rasa-based chatbot, Python 88.5%, last updated April 2023
- `MBAZA-NLP/mbaza-chatbot-orchestrator` — Connects Asterisk PBX to voice pipeline + Rasa, Python, April 2023
- `MBAZA-NLP/bakame-backend-api` — FastAPI speech processing backend, Python, 2023
- `MBAZA-NLP/bakame-frontend` — Web widget, JavaScript, 2023
- `MBAZA-NLP/RW-DEEPSPEECH-API` — Kinyarwanda STT (DeepSpeech), Python, 2023
- `MBAZA-NLP/mbaza-tts-rest-api` — TTS REST API (NVIDIA FastPitch), Python, 2023
- HuggingFace model: `mbazaNLP/bakame-chatbot-kinyarwanda`

**Architecture:** User dials SIP extension 770 -&gt; Asterisk PBX -&gt; Orchestrator (greeting, record speech, STT, Rasa chatbot, TTS, play audio) -&gt; three services: DeepSpeech/Nemo (STT), Rasa (rule-based NLU), NVIDIA FastPitch (TTS). Key: Rasa is rule-based intent classification, NOT an LLM, does NOT generate novel responses.

**Claims vs. Evidence (10 claims assessed):**
- "Built their own LLM": Unsubstantiated. Rasa is not an LLM.
- "Personalized conversations": Partially supported (slot-filling only).
- "&lt;4s response time": Plausible.
- "Localizable + accent-aware": Supported for Kinyarwanda only.
- "Works on any feature phone": Partially supported (needs SIP trunk purchase).
- "Real-time dashboards": Unsubstantiated.
- "Multi-school + multi-district": Unsubstantiated.
- "Government &amp; NGO integrations": Supported for original project (GIZ/Digital Umuganda's work).
- "High-volume telephony": Architecturally plausible but no evidence.
- "Offline-first architecture": Misleading (server needs internet).

**Published Impact Data:** No quantitative learning outcome data published at all. No pre/post test scores, no effect sizes, no completion rates, no engagement metrics, no third-party evaluation, no peer-reviewed publications.

**User Scale:** "Stress-tested in Rwanda" (no details). Planned pilot: 5 schools, 1,000 students (Oct 2025) — no follow-up. Planned 10-20 schools by Dec 2025 — no follow-up. Planned expansion to Senegal/Zambia — no evidence.

**M-Gov Award Context:** UAE government organized, university students worldwide, 3,500+ entries from 74 countries, 60 finalists. Innovation/concept prize — NOT a technical audit. No requirement for published results. Evaluates vision and potential, not verified claims.

**Relationship MBAZA-NLP / Bakame AI:** Both share name "Bakame," target Rwanda, deliver AI content via voice telephony. MBAZA-NLP repos last updated April 2023, before Bakame AI emerged in 2025. MBAZA-NLP developed by GIZ, Digital Umuganda, Mozilla, RISA — not current founders. Founders are undergrads in Economics/Finance and Business Info Systems, not CS/engineering. Most likely interpretation: founders forked/extended MBAZA-NLP stack.

**Statement of Originality — Sabi:** Independently conceived and developed. No shared code, infrastructure, organizational relationship, or IP with Bakame AI or MBAZA-NLP. Detailed comparison table across 15 dimensions showing fundamental differences in country, target population, subject matter, pedagogical framework, AI architecture, STT, TTS, telephony, application framework, curriculum, student memory, evidence base, web platform, language/culture.

**Technical Originality (5 points):** No shared code (Next.js/TypeScript vs Python/Rasa/Asterisk). No shared infrastructure. No shared curriculum. No organizational relationship. Different conception of voice AI tutoring (LLM vs rule-based).

**Prior Art (8 entries):** Interactive Radio Instruction (1970s), Pratham/TaRL (2001), ConnectEd/Youth Impact (2020), Viamo/3-2-1 (2012), Rori AI (2022), Mteja IVR (2018), Eneza Education (2011), World Bank AI Tutoring RCT (2024).

**Legal Precedent (5 principles):** Open-source licensing, no standing to assert IP based on borrowed tech, ideas not copyrightable (17 U.S.C. 102(b)), independent creation defense, functional elements not protectable (Lotus v. Borland).

**Summary — 10 findings:**
1. Bakame claim of "built their own LLM" not supported.
2. Underlying tech developed by GIZ, Digital Umuganda, Mozilla, RISA.
3. No quantitative learning outcome data published.
4. $50K M-Gov Award was concept/innovation prize.
5. Public architecture uses SIP extension dialing, unclear if public number exists.
6. Very early stage — two undergrads, no incorporation, no revenue, no VC, no pilot results.
7. Sabi independently developed, technically original.
8. Sabi uses fundamentally different technology (Claude Haiku LLM vs Rasa).
9. Voice-based education is well-established prior art.
10. Bakame has no legal basis to assert IP claims against Sabi.

(Note: the file also contains informal questions appended at the bottom about AT costs, partnership with AT, kids needed for PoC, GPU deployment, innovative workarounds for telco costs, YarnGPT, Cambridge curriculum vetting, etc.)

### SABI_SECURITY.md

**Server Details:** IP 136.243.8.51. API URL: https://api.eduforequality.org. Frontend: https://sabi.eduforequality.org (Vercel). Provider: Hetzner GEX44 (dedicated bare-metal, Falkenstein, Germany). OS: Ubuntu 22.04 LTS.

**10 Security Measures:**

1. **SSH Access (Key-Only):** Ed25519 SSH key pair. Password auth disabled. PAM auth disabled. KbdInteractive auth disabled. Only root via SSH key. fail2ban installed (caught 70+ brute-force attempts, banned 8 IPs). Key stored at `~/.ssh/id_ed25519` on Naomi's laptop.

2. **Network Firewall:** Only 3 ports open — 22 (SSH), 80 (HTTP, redirects to HTTPS), 443 (HTTPS). Docker containers bound to 127.0.0.1. Ollama LLM accessible only from Docker network. Before fix: ports 8000 and 11434 were publicly accessible.

3. **HTTPS/SSL:** TLS 1.2/1.3 via Let's Encrypt certbot. Nginx reverse proxy (443 -&gt; localhost:8000). Auto-renews via systemd timer every 12 hours.

4. **API Key Authentication:** Protected endpoints (require X-API-Key): POST /stt, /tts, /llm, /process-turn. Open endpoints: GET /health, /voice/* (AT webhooks), /audio/* (pre-generated audio).

5. **Docker Secrets:** API keys/credentials NOT in plaintext env vars. Mounted as encrypted files at /run/secrets/ inside container. 5 secrets: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, YARNGPT_API_KEY, SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY, SABI_API_KEY, AT_API_KEY. Stored in /opt/sabi/sabi-server/secrets/ (chmod 600, root-only).

6. **Rate Limiting:** AI endpoints (expensive): 30 req/min per IP. Webhooks: 120 req/min per IP. General: 60 req/min per IP. HTTP 429 on exceed. Implementation: rate_limiter.py + SecurityMiddleware.

7. **AT Webhook Verification:** Requests to /voice/* verified against AT's AWS eu-west-1 IP ranges. Unknown IPs blocked with HTTP 403. Implementation: at_verify.py + is_africastalking_ip().

8. **Automated Health Monitoring:** Every 5 minutes via cron. Checks sabi-server health, chatterbox-tts health, disk usage (&gt;85% alert), GPU (nvidia-smi), audio cache (auto-cleans &gt;2GB). Auto-recovery: docker compose restart if service down. Logs: /var/log/sabi-health.log (auto-rotated 10MB).

9. **Automated Daily Backups:** 3 AM CET, 7-day retention. Backs up: server code, Docker Compose config, Dockerfiles, secrets, .env, Nginx/SSL configs, SSH keys, Chatterbox TTS/Asterisk configs. NOT backed up: audio_cache (ephemeral), Ollama model weights (re-downloadable), Supabase data (hosted by Supabase). Location: /opt/sabi/backups/sabi_backup_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS.tar.gz. Runs as sabi user.

10. **Dedicated Service User:** sabi user owns .env, secrets/, audio_cache/, backup scripts. Has Docker group access. Backup cron runs as sabi. Health check runs as root.

**Child Safety:** Phone call security handled by AT (standard telecom encryption). Audio only processed in real-time for STT, not stored long-term. Audio files cleaned after 24 hours. Student data in Supabase with RLS. Server accesses via service role key (never exposed to clients). Students identified by phone number, not personal names. No way to query another student's data.

Last updated: March 19, 2026.

### Call with Chris.md

**Incorporation Strategy:**
- Myriad can set up US 501(c)(3) for African companies
- Recommended: Incorporate in Nigeria first, then get 501(c)(3) in US
- Keep Nigerian and US entities separate
- Downside of Nigeria-only: many big funders focused on US-based companies (for-profit)

**Funding &amp; Accelerators:**
- Progress and Grow — focused on impact and product viability
- May be better off incorporating in US — smaller group stands out
- Mastercard Ingini (Mastercard Foundation) — giving away millions in edtech
- Many accelerator companies claim 100% local but not fully true
- Accelerators funded by Dell Foundation, Visa Foundation, others

**Revenue / For-Profit Arm (Luma):** Embedded advertising, paid tier, web-based version, white-label version. All income-generating arms to explore.

**Fundraising Approach:** Out-of-pocket bootstrapping first, then external funds.
Key Lessons: (1) Get people to support your cause — stand out with conviction. (2) Tell your story well. (3) Go to the right spaces. (4) Know your donors. (5) Build relationships. (6) Go deep — research people, don't be discouraged by no's.

Formal letter dated March 25, 2026 to Africa's Talking Ltd requesting SIP trunk connection for use in Nigeria, connected to self-hosted voice server at IP 136.243.8.51.

**Use case:** Children call AT voice number, inbound call routed via SIP to self-hosted server. Sabi greets child by name, delivers interactive lessons. Flash callback model: server initiates outbound calls via SIP so child bears no airtime cost. 5-7 minute calls, 3-5x/week. Covers foundational literacy and numeracy, Cambridge-aligned.

**Scale:** Beginning with ~100 student pilot in Lagos. Expected: 300-500 calls/week, 5-7 min each.

### RESEARCH_BRIEF_SENDABLE.pdf

**Title:** "The Learning Crisis in Nigerian Public Schools -- and How to Fix It." Prepared by Naomi Ivie, February 2026.

**Part 1 — The Scale:**
- 33.3 million children enrolled in public primary schools (UBEC 2022)
- 70% (~23 million) 10-year-olds cannot read a simple sentence (World Bank 2022)
- 18.3 million out-of-school children (UNICEF 2024)
- 343,000-375,000 Lagos public primary pupils (EKOEXCEL Year 5)
- 704,000+ Lagos out-of-school children (LASUBEB 2023-24)
- Pipeline loss: 30M start primary -&gt; 6M reach senior secondary = 80% attrition (UNICEF)

**Part 2 — What Children Actually Know:**
Before EKOEXCEL (2019-2020 baseline):
- 28% of Primary 2-5 pupils couldn't read a single word from Primary 2 passage
- 40% couldn't solve 28-17
- 45% of upper-primary couldn't answer a single reading comprehension question
- Average reading comprehension score: 10%
- Only 12% of pre-primary could solve 8+7

NALABE (43,200 students):
- Everyday math (applying to real situations): 35% — weakest domain at every grade level
- English reading: 41.5% at Primary 5
- Students score higher on recall than comprehension, higher on comprehension than application
- Multiple-choice outperforms constructed responses

Learning-adjusted picture: Nigerian child spends 10.2 years in school, receives equivalent of ~5 years of education. 5.2 years produce zero measurable learning. Harmonized test score: 309/625 — barely above floor of 300.

**Part 3 — Why Schools Are Failing:**
Teacher crisis: 46% teach every subject (no one else). Only 30% have degree + teaching qualification. Teacher attendance: 79% and declining. National shortage: 165,000+ teachers. Pupil-teacher ratio: 37:1 in Lagos, up to 80:1 elsewhere.

Attendance crisis: Pupil attendance 45% (more than half absent any given day). 9% miss entire term for child labor. 6% miss two full terms. Lesson completion rate: only 45% (~8 hours/week instead of 16).

Infrastructure crisis: 31% of schools have no water source. 39% have no toilets. Some schools have 2 textbooks for class of 80. Every Osun school hadn't received new textbooks in 2+ years. One school had zero government-paid teachers.

System efficiency: 45% pupil attendance x 45% lesson completion x 79% teacher attendance = only ~16% of planned learning actually happens.

**Part 4 — Earnings and Poverty:**
Returns to education (Nigeria actual vs global): Per year of primary school 2-4% vs 10%. Per year of secondary 2-4% vs 10%. Per year of university 10-15% vs 15%.

Earnings table (starting from NGN 20K/month, bottom-decile Lagos):
- No school: NGN 20K/mo, NGN 9.6M lifetime
- Primary done, can't read (6 yrs x 2%): NGN 22.5K/mo, NGN 10.8M
- Primary done, CAN read (6 yrs x 8%): NGN 31.7K/mo, NGN 15.2M
- Secondary done, low quality (12 yrs x 3%): NGN 28.5K/mo, NGN 13.7M
- Secondary done, good quality (12 yrs x 8%): NGN 50.4K/mo, NGN 24.2M
- University graduate: NGN 150K-250K/mo, NGN 72-120M

Critical insight: Learning is worth 4x more than attendance. A child who CAN read earns NGN 4.4M more than one who can't — same years, dramatically different life.

What NGN 28.5K/month means: 50kg rice = NGN 70-90K (3 months' salary). Rent: NGN 15-30K/mo. Bus: NGN 10-20K/mo. It is $17/month.

Compared to other countries (monthly earnings after 12 years): Nigeria NGN 28.5K, India NGN 433K, Kenya NGN 330K, Brazil NGN 1.6M, US NGN 6.3M. A Nigerian earns less than an Indian/Kenyan/Brazilian with ZERO education.

GDP cost:
- Annual lost productivity from under-educated workforce: $40 billion (NGN 68 trillion, ~8.5% of GDP) — SBM Intelligence
- Lagos's share: ~NGN 20.4 trillion/year (~2.6% of national GDP)
- If schools worked at global average: +20% labor earnings (World Bank HCI+ 2026)
- Human capital deficits cost: 111% of future earnings — more than double developing-country average

133 million Nigerians (63%) live in multidimensional poverty, education is primary driver.

**Part 5 — What Actually Works:**
- AI tutoring in Nigerian schools (World Bank RCT): 1.5-2 years learning in 6 weeks, $48/student
- Rori AI (WhatsApp math, Ghana): 1 extra year learning, $5/student/year
- ConnectEd/J-PAL (phone tutoring, 6 countries): 0.33 SD, cost of a phone call
- EKOEXCEL (5 years): Non-readers 38% -&gt; 12%, Comprehension 21% -&gt; 60%
- TaRL (Madagascar): 0.56 SD (~2 years gains), ~$10/student
- EIDU (Kenya): 0.52 SD (0.8 extra years literacy)

Cost-effectiveness: AI tutoring 161-260 benefit-cost ratio. Technology-assisted learning ~63:1. Improving school quality 17:1. Best programs: 3 additional learning-adjusted years per $100/child.

**Part 6 — The Solution (Sabi):**
Voice Channel: any phone including feature phones, no connectivity needed, toll-free, NGN 0 cost, reaches ~484,000-684,000 children in Lagos.
App Channel: smartphone $50+ Android, low-bandwidth 3G, NGN 250-1,050/month, reaches ~1.7M children.

Voice-first, app-second. 95% of Nigerian households own a phone, most are feature phones.

How Sabi works: Assesses on first call, teaches one concept per call (Nigerian-context examples), quizzes at start (spaced repetition), adapts in real time (5 right -&gt; harder, 2 wrong -&gt; breaks down further), tracks every child by phone number across sessions. AI gives every child a different lesson at their actual level — that's why AI tutoring produces 1.5-2 years gains.

Current status: Demo built (MIT ABC 2nd prize), curriculum platform deployed, 286+ lessons (47+ hours), foundational voice courses in development, pilot with Lagos schools planned targeting 10 high-burden LGAs.

Nine problems addressed: (1) Foundational Literacy — EXTREME, (2) Foundational Numeracy — EXTREME, (3) Comprehension — HIGH, (4) Instructional Hours — HIGH, (5) Teacher Shortage — HIGH, (6) Attendance — HIGH, (7) Infrastructure — HIGH, (8) Language Barriers — MODERATE, (9) Economic Barriers — MODERATE.

Potential impact: If Sabi shifts children from "low-quality schooling" (3%/yr) to "actual learning" (8%/yr) trajectory: monthly earnings after 12 years goes from NGN 28,516 to NGN 50,362 (+NGN 21,846). Lifetime earnings from NGN 13.7M to NGN 24.2M (+NGN 10.5M). Multiply by millions of children for GDP implications.

**Part 7 — The Pitch Story:** Vivid narrative about a 10-year-old in Lagos. "This is not a story. This is 33 million children."

**Sources:** 20 sources listed including EKOEXCEL Year 5 report, NALABE 2017, Dr. Sonia Ivie report, Osun Landscape Report, Outcomes Fund for OOSC, World Bank, UNICEF, SBM Intelligence, Aromolaran (2004), 6 peer-reviewed RCTs (World Bank AI tutoring, Rori AI, ConnectEd, TaRL, EIDU), WAEC WASSCE (2014-2025), NECO SSCE (2022-2025).

### LAGOS_DATA_REQUEST_BAKAME.pdf

**Title:** "Lagos Data Report: Feasibility &amp; Affordability Assessment — Education for Equality — Sabi Integration Pilot." February 2026.

**Top-Line Summary:** Lagos has ~78% smartphone penetration, ~95% household mobile ownership, but only 45% actively use internet — 50 percentage-point usage gap. 1 GB costs 0.5-0.6% of median salary but 2.0-5.0% of bottom-decile income. ~704,000 out-of-school children, feature phones used by ~22% of mobile owners. Sabi's toll-free, voice-based approach addresses both affordability and device gap.

**Section 1 — Mobile Device Penetration (Lagos):**
- Individual mobile phone (national): 93% (2023, Statista/Afrobarometer)
- Households with mobile (Lagos est.): ~95-97% (2024)
- Smartphone penetration (Lagos): 78% (2024, Vanguard/GSMA)
- Feature phone share (national): 46% of mobile market (Q2 2025, TechCabal/IDC)
- Feature phone share (Lagos est.): ~22% (derived: 100% - 78%)
- Sub-Saharan Africa feature phone/3G users: ~66% (2024, GSMA)
- Nigeria mobile internet usage gap: 120 million without internet despite coverage (2023)
- National smartphone users: 140 million projected (2025, Statista)

**Section 2 — Network Coverage (Lagos):**
- 2G: ~99%, 3G: ~95%, 4G: ~85%, 5G: ~44.6% (55.4% uncovered)
- National connection split: 4G 51.99%, 2G 38.29%, 3G 6.13%, 5G 3.60% (Nov 2025, NCC)
- Lagos internet subscribers by operator: MTN 8.22M (46.7%), Airtel 5.87M (33.4%), Glo 2.96M (16.8%), 9mobile 0.50M (2.8%). Total: 17.59M.

**Section 3 — Active Internet Usage &amp; Usage Gap:**
- National internet users: 107M (Jan 2025, DataReportal)
- National internet penetration: 45.4%
- People NOT using internet: 128M (54.6%)
- Lagos active internet subscribers: 17.59M (Q2 2025, NCC)
- Lagos population: ~17.16M (2025)
- Lagos internet penetration: ~102%* (*multiple SIM cards; effective unique-user penetration ~60-70%)
- National broadband penetration: 51.97% (Dec 2025, NCC)
- Usage Gap (Lagos): ~25-35 percentage points (~4.3-6.0M people covered but not using internet)
- National Usage Gap: ~40 percentage points

**Section 4 — Retail Cost of Mobile Data (Lagos):**
- MTN 1GB/1day: NGN 350. MTN 1GB/7days: NGN 800.
- Airtel 2GB(1+1)/30days: NGN 500 (NGN 250/GB).
- Glo 1GB/1day: NGN 350. 1.5GB/7days: NGN 500. 1.1GB/14days: NGN 750.

- Average monthly salary: NGN 352,000 (~$208, 2025)
- Median worker salary: NGN 60,000 (~$35, 2024)
- National minimum wage: NGN 70,000/month (Jul 2024)
- Bottom-decile monthly income: NGN 15,000-30,000 (2024)
- National poverty rate: 40.1% (2018/projected ~52% by 2026)
- Income below NGN 100K: 26%. No income: 20%.
- Key: massive gap between average (352K) and median (60K) — extreme inequality.

**Section 6 — Affordability Calculations:**
1GB cost (NGN 350) as % of income: Average salary 0.10%, Median 0.58%, Minimum wage 0.50%, Bottom decile 1.75%, Extreme poverty 2.33%.
3GB cost (NGN 1,050) as % of income: Average 0.30%, Median 1.75%, Minimum wage 1.50%, Bottom decile 5.25%, Extreme poverty 7.00%.
ITU/UN benchmark: affordable = &lt;2% of monthly GNI per capita. Bottom decile and extreme poverty exceed this for even 1GB. Sabi's toll-free approach eliminates this cost entirely.

**Section 7 — Target Population Context:**
- Lagos population: ~17.16M. Children 6-14: ~25% = ~4.3M. Children 10-12: ~8% = ~1.37M.
- Out-of-school (Lagos): ~703,756 (6.4%, LASUBEB 2023-24).
- National OOSC: 18.3M (UNICEF 2024). National primary attendance: 63%.
- Children returned to school (Lagos govt effort 2021-2024): 30,000+.
- LASUBEB identified 10 LGAs with highest OOSC concentration — prioritized for Sabi pilot.

**Computed Summary Metrics:**
- Usage Gap (Lagos): ~25-35 percentage points
- Sabi Reachable Population: Feature-phone-only households ~710,000. Children 10-12 per HH: 710K x 0.4 = ~284,000 directly. Plus smartphone households avoiding data costs: ~200-400K additional.
- **Total estimated reachable: ~484,000-684,000 children in Lagos.**

**Affordability Comparison:** 1GB data for learning app: NGN 350 (1.75% of bottom income) vs Sabi NGN 0 (toll-free). 3GB for video content: NGN 1,050 (5.25%) vs Sabi NGN 0. Smartphone + data plan: NGN 15,000+ upfront + NGN 350/mo (78%+) vs Sabi works on any phone.

**Conclusion — Is Sabi Justified in Lagos? Yes -- strongly justified.** Three reasons: (1) Device gap: 22% of Lagos mobile users have feature phones only. (2) Affordability barrier: 1GB costs 1.75-2.33% of bottom-decile income, above ITU threshold. (3) Usage gap: 25-35% of covered population doesn't use internet regularly.

Key caveat: Lagos is Nigeria's best-case scenario. States with lower smartphone penetration (32% rural) and higher OOSC rates (&gt;30% northern states) make the case overwhelming.

### Dr_Sonia_Ivie_Report_ Lagos_State_Special_Schools_For_EOF_2026.pdf

**Title:** "Report — Lagos State Special Needs Schools." Submitted to Chairman, LASUBEB, 27th September 2021. Prepared by NewGlobe.

**Overview:** 1016 public primary schools in Lagos State, 1009 under EKOEXCEL. 7 remaining: 5 Special Needs Schools and 2 Correctional Centers. NewGlobe studied Special Needs Schools to assess EKOEXCEL onboarding possibility. Found 5 Special Needs Schools, 31 Inclusive Units (classrooms within 1009 EKOEXCEL schools), and 2 Correctional Centers under Ministry of Youth and Sports.

**Three categories:**
1. **Inclusive Units (31):** Classrooms within regular EKOEXCEL schools where children with disabilities are accommodated. Within 31 of the 1009 schools.
2. **Exclusive Schools (5):** Only admit children with particular disabilities (Hearing/Visual Impairments). Lagos calls these "Special Needs Schools."
3. **Correctional Centers (2):** Under Ministry of Youth &amp; Sports. Boarding schools for children with pending court cases.

No special curriculum exists for Special Needs Schools — they use the regular Lagos State scheme of work, which has no specific sections for children with special needs/disabilities. Same grade system (ECCDE to Primary 6) but age levels are NOT chronologically compliant.

**Conclusion:** EKOEXCEL could potentially integrate 5 out of 7 Special Needs Schools (including 31 Inclusive Units), bringing total to 1014. Contract addendum needed with NewGlobe/SUBEB.

**Detailed School Tables:**

**A. Exclusive Schools (5):**
- Wesley School I for the Hearing Impaired: Mrs Popoola, Oracle 128341, 8 teachers, NI-P6, 4-20 years
- Wesley School II for the Hearing Impaired: Mrs Ojemola, Oracle 150264, 8 teachers, NI-P6, 4-20 years
- Modupe Cole Memorial Child Care &amp; Treatment Home/School: Tejuosho Rachael Adejoke, Oracle 150674, 11 teachers, P6, 6+ years (up to 79 years old)
- National Orthopedic Special School: Taiwo Emmanuel Dosunmu, Oracle 139093, 7 teachers, NI-P6, 6+
- Atunda Olu School for the Physically and Mentally Challenged: Mrs Oyeniran, Oracle 144244, 6 teachers, NI-P6, 3-25 years

**B. Correctional Centers (2):**
- Girls Correctional Center Idi Araba: Adekoya Sussana Oluwayemisi, Oracle 135021, 1 teacher, N2-P6, 7-15 years
- Boys Correctional Center Oregun: Fernandez Adebisi Adejoke, Oracle 9588, 6 teachers, NI-P6, 6-21 years

**C. Inclusive Schools (31 listed with details):** Full table of all 31 inclusive schools with headteacher names, Oracle IDs, phone numbers, number of teachers (ranging from 1 to 15), available classes, specialist teachers (many have None, some have speech/occupational/vocational therapists), and pupil age groups per class (ranging from 3 to 40+ years in some cases, showing severe age-grade misalignment).

Notable inclusive schools include: Oki Primary School Alimosho, R.C.M. Primary School Ayeteju, Maryland Primary School, L.G. Pry School Igando, All Saint Pry School Ifako Ijaye, Sari Iganmu Pry School Apapa, Bola Memorial Pry School Ikeja, Methodist Primary School Agbowa, Central Pry School Festac Town, Ereko Methodist Primary School, St. Joseph Catholic School Lagos Island, Aganju Aka Pry School 2, L.G. Pry School Ipakodo, Estate Primary School Ogba, R.C.M. Pry School Okunraye, Amuwo Odofin Pry School, G.R.A. Pry School Ogudu, Ore Ofe Pry School, Community Pry School Ajangbadi, Central Pry School Oshodi, Amosun Pry School Agege, Ojuwoye Comm. Pry School, Army Children Pry School, Olisa Primary School Mushin, Anglican Pry Sch Araromi, Ado Pry Sch Eti-Osa, Muslim Pry School Badagry, New Oko Pry Sch Ifako/Ijaye, Local Authority Sch Ajara, Ansar-Ud-Deen Pry Sch Ajara, Methodist Primary Sch Apapa.

**Section 3 — Differences EKOEXCEL vs Special Schools:**
- Age groupings not aligned with regular EKOEXCEL class groupings
- Tools used are different
- Specialist teacher groupings differ across all EKOEXCEL schools

**Section 4 — Challenges:**
- No age-grade sync (7-year-olds in ECCDE alongside 17-year-olds)
- Special schools timetable not aligned with EKOEXCEL timetable
- Specialized teacher training needed for each school category
- Children with varying learning and physical disabilities all in one combined class
- No scheme of work from Lagos State addressing special needs
- Curriculum adaptation challenges (braille lesson plans, sign language, adaptive skills)
- Special tools needed per lesson not captured in current lesson plans
- Correctional centers: transient nature (children stay 3 months to completion, flexible timetables)
- Inclusive schools need audio lesson variations, braille, other delivery formats not in EKOEXCEL

**Section 5 — Recommendations (5 opportunities with caveats/conditions):**
1. Schools classified into grades (N1-P6) so literacy/numeracy classes can launch — but grading doesn't follow age correlations, specialized tools need investigation.
2. Opportunity for proper special needs curricular framework — schools don't currently operate within one.
3. Specialized teacher training across all school categories — teachers with specialized training get transferred to regular schools, creating gaps.
4. Deploy lesson plans for Literacy and Numeracy content — needs specialized formats (audio, braille), Learning Support Assistants in each classroom.
5. Correctional centres promising for onboarding — serve regular children without disabilities, may not need extensive lesson redesign.

**Section 6 — Summary and Next Steps:** Detailed table with count, description, who runs them, and summary of possibilities for each category (Inclusive Units: 31, Exclusive Schools Category 1: 3, Exclusive Schools Category 2: 2, Correctional Centers: 2).

### NALABE 2017 JSS 2.pdf (first 20 pages read)

**Title:** National Assessment of Learning Achievement in Basic Education in Nigeria — Junior Secondary Two. Published by Universal Basic Education Commission (UBEC). ISBN: 978-978-52091-4-3.

**Preface:** History of NALABE: 1st edition 2001, 2nd 2003, 3rd 2006, 4th 2011, 5th (this one) administration November 2017, data analysis March 2018, report finalized May 2018. Signed by Dr. Hamid Bobboyi, Executive Secretary, UBEC.

**Executive Summary:**
- Sampling: 4 stages — 6 LGEAs per state (216 total), 1,080 primary schools, 21,600 primary 6 learners, 3,240 primary 6 teachers, 12,960 parents.
- Used Item Response Theory (IRT) instead of Classical Test Theory for 2017.
- 1,199 Multiple Choice Test responses and 50 constructed response scripts obtained.

**Learners' Home and Support:**
- 88% lived with parents. 73.5% ate breakfast at home. 1-4 meals/day.
- Only 50% owned English Studies and Mathematics textbooks.
- Only 8.7% spent 3+ hours/week on homework.

**Teachers in Schools:**
- 83.4% had 0-20 years experience. 87% of head-teachers were married.
- Only 28.6% of teachers had degrees with teaching qualification.
- 84% of head-teachers attended in-service training in past 5 years.
- 57.40% of teachers taught all subjects.
- Average lesson duration: 21-40 minutes. Teaching weeks/year: 21-40.
- 49.30% gave daily homework. 44.30% spent 1-10 hours/week correcting.
- 80.30% used published textbooks. 15.10% used audio-visual materials.
- 98% liked teaching. Teachers had average/high/low morale.
- Head-teachers disliked: poor salary/delay, poor working environment, lack of societal recognition.

**School and Community:**
- Average school distance: &lt;1 km for 55.5% of learners.
- 70% of schools well-demarcated. 47.2% free from erosion.
- No established water source in 31.1% of schools. Toilets in 60.80%.
- Community support: security (24.70%), land (23.40%), money (12.40%), auxiliary teachers (10.40%).

**Attitude of Learners:**
- English and Mathematics most liked subjects.
- Basic Science/Technology and Social Studies least liked.
- 90.70% liked their teachers.

**Learners' Achievement:**
- **English Studies:** National/zonal/state scores showed no significant differences by gender, location, school type. MCQ scores: good. Essay: only fair. Reading (59.59) higher than grammar (53.66). Comprehension (63.43) higher than Knowledge (56.65) and Higher Order (53.41). Urban MCQ performance higher than rural in most zones.
- **Mathematics:** MCQ good, Essay only fair. By content: Numbers/numeration (64.40) highest, Algebraic process (59.11), Basic Operations (51.04), Everyday Mathematics (34.48) lowest. By cognitive domain: Comprehension (60.47), Knowledge (59.54), Higher Order (54.31). Textbooks correlated with higher scores. Learners traveling 1-2 km performed best. Those with textbooks (60.50) scored higher than without (57.70).
- **Basic Science and Technology:** Fair in both MCQ and Essay. By content: You and Environment (54.42%), Science and Development (51.34%), Living/Non-Living Things (40.86%). Cognitive: Higher Order (54.62%), Knowledge (51.48%), Comprehension (42.20%).
- **Social Studies:** Fairly well in both tests. By theme: Family (69.23), Culture (65.51), National Economy (50.31), Infrastructure (66.89), Health (67.02), People/Environment (60.02), Social Issues/Problems (62.45). Cognitive: Knowledge (62.75), Comprehension (61.80), Higher Order (61.77). MCQ national mean: 49.98. Essay: 49.35.

## ROOT-LEVEL FILES

### SABI_PATH_TO_SCALE.md

**Core Principle:** Focus on kids first. API is year 2-3. Every buyer asks "show me the data." Build proof, then platform. Every platform started as a product (Twilio, Stripe).

**Phase 1: Prove It Works (Now -&gt; Q4 2026):**
- 10-kid think tank: April 15, 2026, Lagos, 2 weeks. Test diagnostic placement, voice clarity, market-math framing, pacing. Key metric: do kids call back the next day unprompted?
- Fix what breaks: May-June 2026
- Legal setup: June-Aug 2026 (501(c)(3), Nigerian CAC)
- Remaining lesson scripts: June-Aug 2026 (Literacy Phase 2, Numeracy Modules 5-6)
- School partnerships: June-Aug 2026. Partner with 10 schools across 5 high-burden LGAs via advisory board (Dr. Sonia/EKOEXCEL, Rhoda Odigboh, Remi Tanimola).
- 150-350 kid pilot: Sept 2026 - Feb 2027. Pre/post TaRL assessment. Target: 0.25-0.35 SD improvement. 5-7 min daily calls, alternating literacy/numeracy.
- Pilot results published: Feb-Mar 2027.
- Cost: ~$2K-5K (MIT prize + Wesleyan grant). Outcome: publishable pilot data.

**Phase 2: Get Funded with Data (Q1-Q2 2027):**
- Foundations: Mastercard, Gates, Ford, Elumelu ($100K-500K)
- UNICEF Venture Fund next round ($100K equity-free)
- Lagos State (LASUBEB) institutional adoption ($500K-2M)
- World Bank HOPE-EDU federal integration ($1M+ from $500M fund)
- MTN/Airtel zero-rating (zero telephony cost)
- Bilateral donors: USAID, DFID, GIZ ($200K-5M)
- One $100K grant funds 5,000-kid deployment. Zero-rating drops per-student cost from ~$23 to ~$1.

**Phase 3: API Emerges Naturally (H2 2027):**
At 5,000+ kids, pipeline proven. Other orgs ask "can we use your voice system?" Wrap pipeline in API. "Sabi for Developers" soft launch (invite-only, 3-5 partners). Pricing: $0.04-0.15/min (undercuts Vapi/Retell by 40-65%, only provider optimized for African markets). Engineering: 3-6 months for API gateway, metering, billing, docs, SDKs.

**Phase 4: Two-Product Platform (2028):**
Sabi for Organizations (managed delivery):
- Pilot (100-500): $10-15/student ($1K-7.5K)
- Mid-scale (1K-50K): $5-8/student ($5K-400K)
- National (100K+): $2-5/student ($200K-500K+)
- State government (500K students): $3-5/student ($1.5M-2.5M)

Sabi for Developers (Voice AI API):
- Starter: $0.04/min (75% margin)
- Pro: $0.07/min (86% margin)
- Enterprise: $0.10-0.15/min (90%+ margin)
- On-prem: $3,000-5,000/mo

Revenue at scale: 1 NGO partner 10K students = $70-112K/mo. 5 partners = $350-560K/mo. Government 1 state = $3.5-5.6M/mo.

**What NOT to Build Now:** API infrastructure, developer docs/SDKs, analytics dashboards, white-label, adult literacy, AP/IB/IGCSE tutoring. All real, none teach kids to read. Build after pilot data.

**The Moat:** Nigerian-accent Whisper (fine-tuned on AfriSpeech-200), Chatterbox TTS with Naomi's voice clone + emotion-aware exaggeration, answer matcher for children's spoken responses, flash callback telephony (zero cost to family), persistent student memory across calls, market-math framing (9x better outcomes, Nature 2025), 240+ voice lesson scripts with embedded emotion tags. Bakame has no LLM. Vapi/Retell can't handle African accents. Viamo is pre-recorded IVR.

**Right Now Priorities:** (1) Get phone call working end-to-end for April 15 think tank. (2) Activate ElevenLabs coupon (deadline April 3). (3) Don't build API infrastructure -- build the thing that teaches kids to read.

### GOVERNMENT_EDTECH_PRICING_RESEARCH.md

**Research Date:** April 1, 2026.

**Section 1 — Baseline Government Per-Pupil Spending:**
- Nigeria: ~$25/year (World Bank, 2019)
- Kenya: ~$14/year
- Liberia: ~$50/year
- Sub-Saharan Africa average: ~$208/year
- India: ~$139/year
- US: ~$15,000-17,000/year
- UK: ~$8,000-10,000/year

Key insight: Nigeria spends only ~$25/pupil/year total on primary. Edtech must be a fraction. But state-level programs (EKOEXCEL, EdoBEST) and World Bank programs operate at much higher budgets.

**Section 2 — Nigeria-Specific Programs:**
- EKOEXCEL: ~1M children, 1,013 schools, 13,000+ teachers. Contract value NOT disclosed. Best estimate: $40-80/pupil/year for NewGlobe technical component.
- EdoBEST: 370K+ children, 1,029 primary + 232 JSS, 16,000 teachers. World Bank $75M. Implied WB component: ~$203/pupil (multi-year, includes infrastructure).
- HOPE-EDU: $552.18M for 29M children, 500K teachers, 65K schools. Simple division: ~$19/pupil. Multi-year, includes 13,000 new classrooms. Results-based financing.
- World Bank AI Tutoring Pilot (Edo): $48/student for 6 weeks. Projected $124/student full year. GPT-4 via Copilot, after-school computer labs. Results: 2 years learning in 6 weeks. Benefit-cost: 161-260x. Most directly comparable to Sabi.
- UBEC 2026: N441.9B (~$280M). 97.3% to salaries. State matching grants increased to N3.3B.

**Section 3 — Other African Programs:**
- NewGlobe Liberia (LEAP): Government $50/pupil, target $100 total. NewGlobe Year 1: $337/pupil, Year 6: $84/pupil, target: $60. Bridge International: $663-1,052/pupil Year 1. New World Bank EXCEL Project: $88.7M for 350K students (~$253/pupil multi-year).
- EIDU (Kenya/Nigeria): $11/student/year. 750K+ learners. Goal: all 12M+ Kenya public pre-primary/primary students by 2027.
- Eneza Education (Kenya): KSh 10/week (~$5/year) via carrier billing. 10M+ learners.
- Rising Academies: Liberia LEAP $100/pupil. Rising On Air: $0.03/learner/year (broadcast). Rori AI: $5/student marginal cost.
- Bridge International: $6/month fees ($72/year), actual costs $108-156/year. Kremer RCT: 53% more learning.
- Ghana 1-Student-1-Tablet: 1.2M SHS students, tablets $80-150/unit.
- South Africa Vodacom e-School: Zero-rated by Vodacom CSR. R110M/year (~$6M).
- Rwanda: World Bank $200M credit. NewGlobe active.

**Section 4 — India Programs:**
- Mindspark/EI: Rs 130/child/year/subject (~$1.55). At government scale uses government infrastructure. Target: $2/student for 2M students. 500K+ students across 17 states. MIT J-PAL RCT: 2-3x learning gains.
- ConveGenius/SwiftChat: 14 states, 100M students targeted, 53 AI chatbots in 13 languages. Free to students.
- DIKSHA: 100% free, government-funded. 36 states/UTs, 19,698 courses, 182.3M enrollments. Built on open-source Sunbird.
- Pratham/TaRL: &lt;$10/child/year. 80M+ children. "Great Buy" by GEEAP.

**Section 5 — US and UK:**
- Khanmigo: $15/student/year (was $60, then $35, now $15). Min 250 licenses. Free for teachers. New Hampshire DOE provides free to all state schools.
- US High-Dosage Tutoring: $1,000-3,000/student/year. Tennessee $500/4th-grader. Louisiana $30M/year. Connecticut $11.5M ESSER.
- UK NTP (now closed): Up to $162-202/pupil (75% subsidized). $18/pupil/hour. 15 hours standard. ~$1.5B over 3 years.
- US EdTech licensing: $5-50/student/year software. AI tutoring premium: $15-60/student/year.

**Section 6 — AI Tutoring Government Pricing:**
- World Bank/Edo: $48 (6 weeks) / $124 (full year)
- Khanmigo US districts: $15/year
- Rori AI: $5/student marginal
- Mindspark India: $1.55/subject/year
- Sabi (projected): $0.24-2.44/year at scale
- **Sabi would be the cheapest AI tutoring program in the world at scale.**

**Section 7 — How Governments Structure Contracts (6 models):**
1. Per-Student Per-Year (most common): NewGlobe $60-100, EIDU $11, Khanmigo $15, Mindspark $1.55
2. Results-Based/Outcomes-Based: HOPE-EDU, US OBC trend
3. Large Block Grant (multi-year): EdoBEST $75M, HOPE-EDU $552M, Liberia $88.7M, Rwanda $200M
4. Government-as-Subscriber: DIKSHA, ConveGenius, Vodacom e-School
5. Per-Student Payment to Private Operators: Pakistan PPRS ~$5, Liberia PSL $50+$50, Bridge $72/year
6. Telco CSR Partnership (zero cost to govt): Viamo 3-2-1, Airtel-UNICEF $57M/5yrs, Vodacom Siyakha

Typical contract lengths: Pilot 6 weeks-1 year. Initial 2-3 years. Scaled 3-5 years. World Bank 4-6 years.

**Section 8 — GEEAP "Smart Buys":**
- Great Buys: Structured pedagogy, TaRL (&lt;$10/child/year), pre-primary programs
- Good Buys: Targeted instruction using technology (including adaptive learning), merit scholarships, remedial education
- Benchmark: "Over three years of high-quality education for as little as $100 per child." SSA average government spending: $208/student.

**Section 9 — RFPs/Procurement:** Very few published RFPs for software-based edtech in Nigeria. Government edtech procurement happens through: (1) direct partnerships (EKOEXCEL model), (2) World Bank project procurement, (3) donor-funded pilots that become embedded.

**Section 10 — Pricing Implications for Sabi:**

Pricing Sweet Spot:
- Ultra-low (grant-funded): $0.25-2/student/year, comparable to Rising On Air, for UNICEF/donors
- Low (government budget): $2-10/student/year, comparable to Rori/Pratham/Eneza, for state boards/UBEC
- Medium (World Bank funded): $10-25/student/year, comparable to EIDU/Khanmigo/HOPE-EDU
- Premium (full managed service): $25-100/student/year, comparable to NewGlobe/EdoBEST

Sabi's unique positioning: (1) $0.24-2.44/student/year at scale -- cheaper than ANY comparable program. (2) No hardware required. (3) No internet required. (4) AI-powered. (5) World Bank Edo pilot validated the model at 161-260x benefit-cost ratio.

Recommended B2G Pricing:
- Phase 2 (with pilot data): $5-15/student/year to state boards and World Bank projects. At 5,000 students = $25K-75K. At 100,000 = $500K-1.5M.
- Phase 3 (at scale, proven outcomes): $2-5/student/year to federal programs. At 1M = $2M-5M. At 10M = $20M-50M.

```
Create a 12-slide pitch deck for "Education for Equality / Sabi" — a nonprofit that teaches Nigerian children to read through AI-powered phone calls. SLIDE 1 — TITLE
"SABI" in large bold type. Tagline: "Teaching Nigeria's children to read — through a phone call." Subtitle: Education for Equality | Naomi Ivie, Founder. Show a warm image of Nigerian children or a phone.

SLIDE 2 — THE PROBLEM (Personal Story + Crisis)
Title: "33 million children enrolled. 23 million can't read."
Key stats: 70% of Nigerian 10-year-olds cannot read a simple sentence (World Bank). 18.3 million out of school — highest in the world (UNICEF). Only 16% of planned learning actually happens. Nigeria loses $40 billion/year in productivity (CSEA/IDRC). Speaker note: founder's personal story — grew up in Lagos, family slept in a Volkswagen, saw children hawking in traffic, no difference between her and them except opportunity.

SLIDE 3 — THE INSIGHT
Title: "The answer is already in their pocket."
55% of Nigerians can't afford internet. Every education app requires a smartphone and data. But 95% of households own a phone. 99% have 2G coverage. The infrastructure to reach every child already exists — it's a phone call.

SLIDE 4 — THE SOLUTION (Overview)
Title: "Sabi — 'to know' in Nigerian Pidgin"
An AI tutor accessed by calling a phone number. No smartphone. No internet. No cost to the family. Child dials → Sabi answers, asks their name → runs diagnostic through conversation → teaches at THEIR level → 5-7 min call, one concept, Nigerian market scenarios → child hangs up → calls back tomorrow → Sabi remembers everything. Multiple kids on one family phone each get their own profile.

SLIDE 5 — HOW IT TEACHES (Details)
Title: "Children solve market math 9x better than abstract math"
Show a mock conversation: "You buy pure water for 20 naira and groundnuts for 30 naira. How much altogether?" (Nature 2025, Sokoto Nigeria, n=5,997: 48.5% vs 5.4%). Sabi uses Teaching at the Right Level (TaRL) — proven across 7 African countries. If they get 3 right, difficulty increases. If they struggle, Sabi scaffolds — never says "wrong." Persistent memory across calls. 240 lesson scripts across literacy and numeracy.

SLIDE 6 — THE EVIDENCE
Title: "This approach is already proven."
Table: World Bank AI tutoring (Nigeria) = 1.5-2 years of learning in 6 weeks, $48/student. Rori AI (Ghana) = 1 extra year of learning, $5/student. ConnectEd (6 countries) = 0.33 SD improvement, $12/student. XPRIZE (Tanzania) = reading 7% to 45% in 15 months, no teachers. EKOEXCEL (Lagos) = non-readers 38% to 12%. Benefit-cost ratio of AI tutoring: 161-260 — highest of ANY education intervention ever measured (World Bank).

SLIDE 7 — COMPETITION LANDSCAPE
Title: "The only voice AI tutor built for Africa"
Comparison: Khan Academy (needs smartphone + internet), Rori AI (needs WhatsApp), World Bank AI (needs supervised computer lab), EKOEXCEL (school-only, 45% attendance), Bakame AI Rwanda (rule-based scripts, no LLM). Sabi: works on any $5 phone, no internet, adaptive AI with persistent memory. First conversational AI tutor delivered over basic voice calls in Africa. Distribution cost is near zero — a phone number spreads itself.

SLIDE 8 — COST
Title: "$18/student today. $0.41 at scale."
Table showing cost collapse: 300 students (pilot) = $18.16/student. 5,000 students = $11.70. 5,000 + telco zero-rating = $0.41. 1,000,000 = $0.02. Compare: World Bank $48, EKOEXCEL $24, ConnectEd $12, Rori $5, Sabi $0.41. Entire AI stack (speech recognition, language model, text-to-speech) runs on one GPU server at $200/month. Zero API costs. Only variable cost is telephony — eliminated by telco partnerships.
```
